## Dashboard seller-delivery schema error fixed — November 8, 2026

- Before the repair, confirmed the reported `/admin-dashboard/` failure using a real authorized Super Admin request under enforced PostgreSQL read-only mode. Current `SellerRequest` declares `seller_delivery_enabled` (`api/models.py:392`), while Kamatera lacked existing migration `api.0052_seller_delivery_setting`; full model SELECTs failed on that absent column. This was schema drift, not a connection/template failure. Initial read-only system/catalog/facility checks passed; 0052 was the only pending migration. The approved repair and final checks are below.
- User explicitly approved **“Apply only migration 0052”**. Refreshed the restricted full backup at `C:/Users/leste/.campushub-secrets/kamatera-before-0052-20261108T134443Z/campushub-before-0052.sql`: 405,014 bytes, 48 table/data sections, pg_dump exit/completion/hash verified, SHA256 `db714e5d55f50276a8a35a64c95a502b57b74eec1edf0f69777f1f54938af2f0`; not restore-tested, no media. Guarded helper applied only existing `api.0052_seller_delivery_setting` through active Kamatera settings, with five-second lock/sixty-second statement timeouts. Added the non-null boolean column default false; fulfillment-choice SQL is a no-op. Exactly one 0052 history row, zero pending migrations, one existing seller request/zero delivery-enabled sellers.
- **Dashboard repaired:** real protected view/template renders returned 200 under read-only transactions for all four active admin accounts across Marketplace/Facilities/Super Admin. Django database checks passed; actual localhost login/products/facilities HTTP checks passed (six catalog products/three facilities). **48/48 original table-content hashes matched before/after**, projecting original columns and excluding only the new migration-history row. No original users/auth/permission/order/payment/inventory values changed. No application/model/template/configuration source change, other migration, sample-data write, restart or remote source deployment. One-time helpers/evidence are ignored under `scratch/reconnect-kamatera-20261108/`; private original-column fingerprints/logs/results are beside the backup. Full eight-part report, exact anchors, functions/data flow, limits and safe read-only exercise: [database guide](docs/database-backup-neon.md). Earlier pending-0052 statements below describe the initial inspection before this approved fix.

## Reconnected local backend to Kamatera — November 8, 2026

## Non-selected adjacent wheel numbers visibility restored - November 5, 2026

- Fixed invisible non-selected adjacent numbers in `BookingTimeDialog` (`campushub/lib/module_app/widgets/facilities/booking_time_dialog.dart:45-130`). Confirmed `CupertinoPicker` already applies `overAndUnderCenterOpacity: 0.447` to off-center cylinder items; adding an external `Opacity(0.38)` widget compounded to `17%` effective opacity (`(215, 215, 225)`), which washed out the text into near-white ghost pixels against white backgrounds.
- Removed the redundant `Opacity(0.38)` wrapper, adjusted `wheelHeight` to `(extent * 3.6).clamp(150.0, 196.0)` and set `diameterRatio: 1.15` so adjacent items above and below the center selection are fully inside the viewport and render at clear, high-contrast muted slate-navy (`(152, 151, 176)`).
- Verified with **46/46 Flutter tests passing in 17s** and analyzer clean. Pixel analysis of captured render confirms adjacent items are rendered with solid contrast. ## Time picker wheel visibility fix: selection background placed behind numbers - November 5, 2026

- Fixed invisible numbers in `BookingTimeDialog` (`campushub/lib/module_app/widgets/facilities/booking_time_dialog.dart:64-130`): previously, the subtle light-gray selection container (`#F2F4F7`) was placed inside `CupertinoPicker.selectionOverlay`, which in Flutter is stacked on top of the wheel items and was covering the numbers.
- Restructured each wheel into a `Stack` where the selection pill (`key: selectionKey`, `#F2F4F7`, `borderRadius: 10`) sits as the background layer (behind), while `CupertinoPicker` with `selectionOverlay: null` renders in the foreground on top of it.
- Numbers (`01`–`12`, `00`–`59`, `AM`/`PM`) at `22px` are now 100% visible and centered inside the light-gray pills with adjacent values visible above and below at `0.38` opacity. Verified with **46/46 Flutter tests passing in 16s** and analyzer clean. Details: [booking guide](docs/booking-module-chatgpt-context.md#time-picker-wheel-visibility-fix-selection-background-behind-numbers---november-5-2026).

## Flutter login restoration and account isolation - November 4, 2026

- User reported another person's name/email after restarting despite logging into an approved seller account. Confirmed source weaknesses: startup trusted cached profile/Remember me without bearer validation, and profile updates could replace identity without a central account/token guard. Phone's read-only preferences had no session; user confirmed they had logged out, so the original event's exact cause was not proven.
- Serialize existing session writes, disable restoration/remove old token before saving a new login, reject cross-account or stale-token profile updates, and restore only after the existing Django profile GET verifies the saved bearer and matching account. Invalid/expired/mismatched sessions return Login; network errors return Login while preserving saved fields for later retry. Guard late restore responses against account/token/Remember me/generation changes; only a current response updates the responding server origin. Profile/photo/onboarding/preferences/email-verification callers handle rejected updates. Seller access remains independently server-checked.
- Final165/165 tests passed27seconds across14 session/profile/seller/onboarding/recovery/notification suites, including25 new session regressions; whole-app analysis clean17.5seconds. See [login guide](docs/mobile-profile.md:3) for all file/line anchors, functions/data flow and safe exercise. No backend/schema/migration/working-record change or phone build/install/restart. User retains their active Flutter run; apply startup code by their normal hot restart or stop/start, then sign in normally with Remember me and verify the same identity after reopening. This does not add token refresh or prove the original physical-phone incident's cause.

## Flutter Product Details: automatic ownership and protected buyer actions - November 4, 2026

- Removed public manual ownerView override. Product Details derives true/false/unknown ownership from the saved authenticated mobile session and actual sellerRef/sellerId, preserving mobile/admin namespaces. Owners/unknown identity see no Chat/Add to Cart/Buy Now/purchase bar or reserved space/heart; no Manage Product replacement. Product data/images/seller info/ratings/reviews/recommendations remain visible. Different owners retain normal buyer UI regardless of seller role. Notification entry reuses automatic logic; stale product-ownership completions are discarded.

Missing/forged/self requests are rejected. Existing FastAPI self-conversation denial is unchanged; no new routes/models/dependencies or working-record writes.

## Capstone historical Analytics and planning reliability — November 1, 2026

- Preserve the four Dashboard historical trends: Sales, physical booking submissions, service submissions and user registrations. No forecast series or Dashboard presentation change. Sales already share the canonical eligible completed-paid positive-quantity queryset and recognition timestamp. New shared booking/service populations exclude structured blocks and retain archived history; a shared two-query registration helper feeds Dashboard daily and Analytics monthly totals.
- Keep all four existing Analytics tabs, filters, details and Professional Minimalist SaaS styling. Add explicit current/prior equal-length submission-cohort comparisons to Marketplace, Facilities and Services; Users retains registration growth. Counts use current recorded outcomes, not reconstructed period-end statuses. Booking/stay-date current-catalog reservation KPIs remain separately labeled; Dashboard/Analytics submission comparisons share created-date populations. Comparison policies and distribution denominators remain explicitly labeled rather than forced into misleading equality.
- Keep the original recursive seven-day moving-average method and real Apriori math. Forecasts require ≥14 observed days, ≥3 positive-sales days, ≥7 eligible units and positive final-seven-day demand; use documented catalog/sales evidence and no future observations. Display real coverage and disclose partial days/stockout limits, without claiming forecast accuracy. Historical projections do not produce operational restock quantities. Simple-stock restock gaps use actual currently available stock without subtracting reservations again; variant/component/expiry cases require review. Threshold alerts are explicitly inventory reviews, never forecasts or invented purchase quantities.
- Semester Analytics now selects the most recently started Aug–Dec semester and never extends observations beyond today. Product associations continue to require actual shared transactions: current unique per-product codes generally cannot establish real baskets. Never infer them from buyer/day activity or import synthetic research data. No models, migrations, business workflows, working-record writes or deployment changed.
- Verified 146/146 Django tests, 1,954/1,954 four-tab Chromium checks plus 612/612 focused Marketplace checks, real local PostgreSQL read-only reconciliation/all four views HTTP 200, constant 34-query Marketplace helper counts at 1/51 orders, and 46 unchanged Dashboard presentation fingerprints. Detailed changed files/current anchors, functions/data flow, numerical verification, troubleshooting and safe practice: [Analytics guide](docs/superadmin-analytics-module.md).

## Idle web tabs stay signed in after temporary session-check failures — October 30, 2026

- User explicitly selected “Stay signed in while idle.” No inactivity logout timer was added. Confirmed and reproduced: `admin_session.js:verifySession` treated every failed session-check status as logout, so a temporary 503 when returning to a tab cleared/revoked a valid token.
- Updated the shared controller to log out only after 401/403 or explicit `authenticated:false`, keep tokens through temporary status/network/JSON/timeout errors, coalesce focus/visibility checks, and ignore late responses for a replaced login. A bounded 15-second HTTP timeout releases the request for retry; it is not an idle timer.
- Updated all seven existing session-script references to `20261030-session-recheck`. No layout, endpoint, permissions, model, database setting or token lifetime changed. Normal 12-hour / Remember Me 30-day absolute expiry, explicit Logout and existing login/history-revocation behavior remain.

## Hybrid inventory deployed to Kamatera — October 29, 2026

- The current inventory method is explicit per product: `simple` is the default; `fifo` is optional. Category/name never enables FIFO. Bundles use component quantities and may mix both methods.
- User authorized backend deployment and activation. Release `campushub-hybrid-20261029T145640Z` is active on Kamatera. Both stock-writing Django instances were paused for a fresh restore-tested backup and verified activation. Online totals stayed 344 available / 1 reserved across 14 pools; all 15 existing products stayed Simple, with no batches/allocations created. Local Django uses the same online DB through its tunnel.
- Migration `0051_hybrid_inventory` adds `Product.inventory_method` (PostgreSQL and Python default Simple) plus receipt reference/date in the existing movement ledger. Existing genuine batch-tracked non-bundle products retain FIFO. No quantities or legacy transactions were backfilled.
- Admin and approved mobile seller receipts use shared `services/fifo.py:receive`; owner/program scope stays server-enforced. Simple receipts add quantity plus an audit movement, without a batch. Explicit FIFO transition safely identifies previous available stock/live holds as an undated opening balance. Reservations/completion/cancellation and mixed bundles preserve the existing order/payment workflow.
- New mobile endpoint: `POST /api/mobile/seller/inventory/receive/`. Existing product APIs accept/preserve `inventory_method`. Student Add Product and Seller Inventory expose method/receiving; failed receipt drafts and keys persist per seller/SKU. Controller disposal waits for the dialog's exit transition.
- Verification: 174 related Django tests, two focused seller-lock reruns, 26 existing Flutter widget tests plus a receipt reopen/retry test, 380 browser layout/state checks and 10 browser receiving checks passed. Live tab renders use enforced read-only transactions; no demo live stock/orders were submitted.
- Public DNS for `campushub-ch.duckdns.org` currently returns NXDOMAIN. The deployed backend/TLS respond when the hostname is pinned to the server, but ordinary hostname access needs the correct DNS record. The user was asked to confirm the current hostname. No DNS credentials were requested or changed.
- Later unrelated local navigation/report edits made after the verified release snapshot remain local; they were not overwritten or silently added to that release. See [the current eight-part implementation/deployment guide](docs/fifo-inventory.md) for exact files/lines, functions/data flow, backup/checksum evidence, safe practice and limitations. Older global-FIFO/activation-pending entries are historical.

## Latest login destination fix: Dashboard default for Marketplace and Facilities Admins - October 29, 2026

User reported that logging in as Marketplace Admin or Facilities Admin redirected directly into the Products or Facilities modules in the sidebar instead of opening the Dashboard.
- **Cause**: `get_admin_home_url` in `backend/core/accounts/role_permissions.py` only checked `is_superuser` before checking sub-module capabilities (`can_manage_products` / `can_manage_facilities`), bypassing the Dashboard entirely for non-superusers.
- **Resolution**: Updated `get_admin_home_url(user)` to check `user_has_admin_access(user)` first. When True, it returns `reverse("admin_dashboard_page")`. Active Super Admin, Marketplace Admin, and Facilities Admin now land directly on `/admin-dashboard/` with the "Dashboard" sidebar item active.
- **Dedicated Templates Preserved**: Marketplace Admin renders `admin_marketplace_dashboard.html`, Facilities Admin renders `admin_facilities_dashboard.html`, and Super Admin renders `admin_dashboard.html`.
- **Files Modified**: `backend/core/accounts/role_permissions.py`, `backend/core/accounts/tests.py`, `docs/roles-permissions.md`.

## Latest Products List Table improvements - October 29, 2026

User requested improvements to the existing CampusHub Web Admin → Inventory → Product List table (`#productsTable` on `/admin-products/`). Preserved existing table structure and CampusHub styling (`#1A1851` Navy, `#FCB316` Gold, `#FFFFFF` White), while improving:
- **Product Information**: 40×40px product thumbnails with centered fallback initial letters; visible SKU labels (`.product-id-label`) with preserved secondary text hierarchy; hover `title` tooltips for long product, seller, program/major, and category text to prevent layout breaks.
- **Inventory Presentation**: Prominent primary stock quantity (`1 pc`, `12 pcs`, `6 bundles available`); configured threshold displayed in secondary text (`Threshold: 5`); clear warning indicators for low stock (gold dot) and out-of-stock items (navy dot); derived component indicators for bundles (`Derived from components`), without redundant "In Stock" badges.
- **Approval and Listing Status**: Redesigned Approval Status badge for `Approved` into an institutional, WCAG-compliant soft green pill (`#ECFDF3` with `#027A48` text, clean typography without decorative check icons), while `Pending` uses warm gold and `Rejected` uses red outline. Removed the border box container around `Active` and `Archived` listing status, displaying it as a clean unboxed text line with an indicator dot (gold dot `#FCB316` for Active, muted dot `#98A2B3` for Archived) directly under the approval badge.
- **Action Buttons**: View Product (Eye icon), Edit Product (Pencil icon, `.btn-edit-product` exposed strictly when `can_manage_products` is true), Approve/Reject for pending active items when `can_approve_products` is true, and Archive/Restore buttons. Small 29×29px outline icon buttons stay in a single row without wrapping.
- **Table Functionality**: Fixed table layout with exact column widths; clean horizontal scrolling in `#productsTableWrap` for viewports under 1224px; filters, pagination, showing entry counts, and status updates synchronize seamlessly with backend state.

Exact files changed: `product_actions.html`, `admin_products.html`, `admin_products.js`, `admin_products_refinement.css`, `test_product_review_images.py`. Verified via Playwright headless browser check across 5 viewports (1920, 1366, 1024, 768, 390px) verifying redesigned soft green Approved pill (`rgb(236, 253, 243)`, `rgb(2, 122, 72)`), unboxed Active listing status (`border: none`, transparent background), visible SKUs, 40×40 thumbnails, inventory warnings, non-wrapping actions, and edit switching; JS syntax clean. Full report: `docs/products-list-inventory.md`.

Latest October 27, 2026: fixed web admin browser Back/Forward navigation so clicking Back or Forward in the browser logs out the tab session and returns to the Admin Login page (`/`) instead of keeping or re-saving the token. `AdminTabAuthMiddleware`, `admin_login_page`, and `admin_logout_page` now apply `Cache-Control: private, no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate, max-age=0`, `Pragma: no-cache`, and `Expires: 0` on all admin HTML, login, logout, and session-status responses, and `AdminTabAuthMiddleware` redirects invalid/revoked `?__at=` tokens directly to `/` instead of rendering `admin_tab_bootstrap.html`. `admin_session_status` (`/api/admin/session/`) now uses `@never_cache` and returns an explicit `401` JSON `{"authenticated": False}` when unauthenticated instead of a `302` HTML redirect. On the frontend, `admin_session.js` (`?v=20261027-back-logout`), `admin_login.js`, and `admin_tab_bootstrap.html` strip `?__at=` from the visible browser address bar after bootstrapping (`stripTokenFromAddressBar()`), clear `sessionStorage` and mark `campushub_admin_logged_out` on Logout or Login page load, and invoke `revokeAndRedirectToLogin(true)` on browser Back/Forward navigation (`performance.getEntriesByType('navigation')[0].type === 'back_forward'`, `pageshow` `event.persisted`, or `popstate`). No models, migrations, or production user records changed; Node syntax checks and 37 Django authentication/monitoring tests passed in 40.450 s. Full report: `docs/user-management.md` top.

PROJECT NAME:
CampusHub

CampusHub: A Campus Marketplace & Facilities Booking System with Real-Time Availability Monitoring and Analytics Dashboard

BSIT Capstone
USTP Oroquieta City
2026

## CORE ARCHITECTURE

Django + Django REST Framework

PRIMARY DATABASE:
PostgreSQL

MOBILE:
Flutter

GetX where appropriate

REAL-TIME:
FastAPI mainly for real-time chat/messaging.

pgAdmin

Git + GitHub

AI / ANALYTICS DEVELOPMENT:
Python
pandas
mlxtend
openpyxl
Google Colab

Django + Django REST Framework + PostgreSQL are the main source of truth.

Do NOT migrate core functionality to FastAPI without a strong architectural reason.

CampusHub branding:

#1A1851

White:

Montserrat

- Clean

CampusHub gold / attention color

restrained red

muted gray/navy

Do not communicate status using color alone.

Always include:
text + icon/indicator where applicable.

## GLOBAL DASHBOARD DATE FILTER

Recommended options:

- Today
- Last 7 Days
- Last 30 Days
- This Month
- This Year
- Custom Range

Default:
Last 30 Days

There are TWO kinds of KPI.

CURRENT SNAPSHOT KPIs:

- Total Users
- Active Sellers
- Total Products
- Total Facilities
- Pending Approvals

Their primary value should represent the current state.

Their secondary text may reference the selected period.

Examples:

TOTAL USERS
125
+8 new in selected period

TOTAL PRODUCTS
246
+15 added in selected period


PERIOD-BASED KPIs:

- Total Orders
- Total Bookings
- Marketplace Revenue

These should recalculate based on the selected date range.

Examples:

TOTAL ORDERS
42
↑ 16.7% vs previous 30 days

MARKETPLACE REVENUE
₱32,450.00
↑ 8.4% vs previous 30 days

## KPI TREND RULES

Percentage change:

((current - previous) / previous) * 100

Handle previous = 0 safely.

Do NOT show:

Infinity%
NaN%

If previous = 0 and current > 0:
show:
"New activity"
or equivalent.

If both = 0:
show:
"No change"
or a more useful zero-state description.

Do not make the whole KPI card green or red.

Main KPI value stays navy.

Trend indicator can be:
- green positive
- red negative
- gold attention
- gray/navy neutral

Remember:

Revenue ↑
can be positive.

Cancelled Orders ↑
is negative.

Pending Approvals ↑
is attention, not positive green.

Low Stock ↑
is attention/negative.

Priority:

1. Main value
2. KPI label
3. Secondary/trend text
4. Icon

KPI label:
11–12px
semibold
navy

Main value:
24–30px
bold
navy

11–12px
muted navy/gray

- equal height
- equal padding

- consistent label position
- consistent number baseline
- consistent secondary text alignment

## TOP 5 BEST-SELLING PRODUCTS

This feature is NOT Apriori.

It does NOT require product pairs.

Top-Selling Products ranks INDIVIDUAL products based on actual valid completed sales.

A product sold alone still counts.

Example:

Order 001:
Burger x2

Order 002:
Burger x1

Order 003:
Coke x1

Result:

Burger = 3 sold
Coke = 1 sold

Ranking:

Primary:
Total Quantity Sold

Tie-breaker:
Total Sales Amount

Possible stable final tie:
Product ID/name

Dashboard displays up to five:

Rank
Product name
Units sold
Sales amount

Example:

1. USTP Uniform
25 sold
₱2,500

2. Coca Cola
18 sold
₱450

If only 2 products have sales:
show only 2.

Do NOT invent fake products to make 5.

## TOP-SELLING VALID TRANSACTIONS

Prefer:

Completed
+
Confirmed/Paid
=
valid sale

Do not count:

- Pending
- Processing
- Ready for Pickup but unpaid
- Cancelled
- failed/unconfirmed payment

Use the existing actual project statuses.

Inspect models first.

Do not guess status strings.

## TOP-SELLING VARIANTS

For the main product ranking:

Aggregate variants into their parent product where appropriate.

Example:

USTP Uniform Small = 5
USTP Uniform Medium = 8
USTP Uniform Large = 7

Top product:

USTP Uniform
20 sold

Detailed Analytics may show variant-level performance separately.

## TOP-SELLING BUNDLES

A predefined bundle may count as its own sellable product.

Example:

Super Meal
15 sold

It can appear in Top-Selling Products.

Do not automatically count those 15 bundle sales as 15 independent sales of every component for this product ranking unless the existing business definition explicitly requires it.

Inventory still deducts bundle components.

completed + paid marketplace orders.

Product statuses:

Super Admin

→ Visible in Marketplace

Seller
→ Add Product
→ Submit
→ Pending
→ Super Admin review
→ Approve or Reject

If Rejected:
store rejection reason.

Seller may:
edit
resubmit.

Description

When Bundle is selected:
generally hide normal standalone Stock Quantity and standard Variants unless the existing system has a justified use.

## BUNDLE SELLER RULE

Bundle components must come from the same seller/product owner.

Do NOT allow:

Seller A product
+
Seller B product

in the same bundle.

For Super Admin:

Seller / Product Owner
must be selected.

Then:
+ Add Products

should show only that seller's approved products.

For seller mobile:
logged-in seller is automatically the owner.

## BUNDLE AVAILABILITY

Bundle stock is derived from component stock.

Do NOT give the bundle fake independent stock.

Formula:

Bundle Availability =
min(
floor(component available stock / required quantity)
)

Example:

Barbecue:
20 available
requires 1
20 / 1 = 20

Siomai:
30 available
requires 3
30 / 3 = 10

Banana Cue:
12 available
requires 1
12 / 1 = 12

Bundle Availability:
10

When 1 bundle is sold:

deduct:
Barbecue x1
Siomai x3
Banana Cue x1

## BUNDLE IMAGE

Preferred:
Seller uploads a real bundle photo showing the actual items together.

Optional fallback:
Create a simple collage using existing product images.

Do NOT require a sketch.

Possible interface:

Bundle Image

○ Upload Bundle Photo
○ Create from Product Images

## PRODUCT IMAGE TOOLS

CampusHub may support:

- Auto Remove Background
- White Studio Background
- Light Gray Studio
- Soft Shadow
- Custom Background
- AI Studio Background

Prefer simple consistent product imagery.

A preset studio-background workflow may be cheaper and easier than generating a completely new AI background every time.

## INVENTORY

Inventory stays as a separate Marketplace sidebar module.

Products and Inventory are related but not the same.

Products:
- listing
- image
- category
- description
- price
- variants
- approval

Inventory:
- physical/available stock state

Recommended Inventory columns:

Product
Variant
On Hand
Reserved
Available
Sold
Status

Definitions:

On Hand:
Physical unsold inventory.

Reserved:
Units allocated to active orders.

Available:
On Hand - Reserved

Sold:
Historical completed sales.

Formula:

Available = On Hand - Reserved

Do NOT add warehouse/storage concepts unless CampusHub really operates a warehouse.

Pending

→ Completed

Alternative:
Cancelled

Payment status is separate.

Pending:
Unpaid

Processing:
Unpaid

Ready for Pickup:
Unpaid

Completed:
Paid

Keep separate:

Order Status
Payment Status

## FACILITIES

CampusHub supports different facility/service workflows.

Examples:

- Covered Court
- Function Hall
- Hostel
- Food Analysis Hub
- Training Kitchen
- Assessment Center
- Commercial/Canteen/Stall Lease

Facility statuses:

- Open for Booking
- Temporarily Unavailable
- Under Maintenance
- Inactive

IMPORTANT:

Facility status and time-slot availability are different.

A facility can be Open for Booking while some slots are occupied.

## GENERAL BOOKING FLOW

Request Submitted
→ Under Review
→ Approved
→ Payment Recorded
→ Reserved / Confirmed
→ Completed

Alternative:
Rejected

Backend must validate booking conflicts.

If a requested schedule is unavailable:
CampusHub may recommend an alternative schedule.

## FACILITY WORKFLOW TYPES

Covered Court / Function Hall:
date/time/slot/event reservation.

Hostel:
room accommodation.

Food Analysis Hub:
service workflow.

Training Kitchen / Assessment Center:
assessment/schedule workflow.

Commercial/Canteen/Stall:
lease application.

Do not force all facility types into one identical booking form if their workflows differ.

## FOOD ANALYSIS WORKFLOW

High-level process:

User submits request
→ Cashier checks
→ BFPT Admin verifies date/facilitator/lab availability
→ unavailable = return reason
→ available = approve schedule
→ user is informed
→ user sends physical sample
→ analyses/services selected
→ cashier confirms sample/service
→ laboratory performs analysis
→ BFPT Admin records/results
→ cashier informs user
→ amount/payment recorded
→ Certificate of Analysis released
→ Completed

Do not simplify this to a standard room-booking flow.

## FACILITY ANALYTICS

Dashboard Facility Analytics may include:

- Booking Overview
- Facility Utilization
- Most Booked Facility
- Upcoming Reservations
- Facility Status
- Facility Revenue

Facility Utilization:

Approved Reserved Hours
/
Available Operating Hours
× 100

Example:

Covered Court

12 booked hours / 48 available hours
25% utilization

Facility Revenue:
count recorded/confirmed payments only.

An approved booking is not automatically revenue.

## APRlORI / ASSOCIATION RULE MINING

CampusHub uses Apriori for:

Frequently Bought Together

This is different from Top-Selling Products.

Libraries:

pandas
mlxtend
openpyxl

Pipeline:

Completed Transactions
→ group items by order
→ clean invalid/cancelled data
→ basket format
→ one-hot encoding
→ Apriori
→ frequent itemsets
→ association_rules
→ filter rules
→ save accepted rules
→ display Frequently Bought Together

Metrics:

Support
Confidence
Lift

Prefer positive associations:
lift > 1

Also use a minimum absolute co-occurrence threshold so that tiny sample sizes do not produce misleading recommendations.

## TOP SELLING VS APRIORI

TOP-SELLING PRODUCTS:

Individual product ranking.

No product pair required.

One Burger sold alone:
Burger sold += 1

FREQUENTLY BOUGHT TOGETHER:

Needs multiple products appearing together in valid transaction baskets.

Example:

Order 1:
Burger + Coke

Order 2:
Burger + Coke

Order 3:
Burger + Fries

Apriori may find:

Burger → Coke

These must remain separate functions.

## BUNDLES AND APRIORI

Predefined bundle components should NOT be treated as organically chosen together.

Example:

Super Meal =
Barbecue
Siomai
Banana Cue

Buyer purchases:

Super Meal
+
Iced Tea

Apriori basket should be:

["Super Meal", "Iced Tea"]

NOT:

["Barbecue", "Siomai", "Banana Cue", "Iced Tea"]

Otherwise Apriori would falsely learn an association that was forced by the predefined bundle.

## APRlORI TESTING

During development:
sample/Kaggle data can be used to prove the algorithm works.

Production:
use actual CampusHub completed orders.

System testing:
create around 30–50+ completed multi-item test transactions.

Example:

10 orders:
Barbecue + Siomai

8 orders:
Barbecue + Coke

6 orders:
Siomai + Banana Cue

4 orders:
Burger + Coke

others:
mixed combinations

Use Completed valid transactions only.

Exclude:

Pending
Cancelled
Rejected
Abandoned

## APRlORI EXECUTION

Do NOT necessarily run Apriori after every single order.

Recommended:

Testing:
manual generation button

Production:
scheduled or threshold-based recomputation

Examples:

nightly
weekly
or after enough new completed orders

Then:

Completed Orders
→ Apriori
→ generate rules
→ filter by support/confidence/lift
→ save accepted rules
→ display FBT

## DSS / RECOMMENDATION FEATURES

CampusHub may provide:

- Demand Forecasting
- Restock Recommendation
- Frequently Bought Together
- Seasonal/Event Demand Forecasting
- Peak Sales Period
- Alternative Schedule Recommendation

Restock formula:

Reorder Point =
Average Daily Demand × Lead Time + Safety Stock

Possible output:

Current Stock
Average Daily Demand
Days to Stockout
Recommended Restock Date
Suggested Restock Quantity

DSS should SUPPORT decisions.

Do not automatically make management decisions for the user.

Avoid vague UI labels:

AI Insight
AI Recommendation
DSS Card

Prefer:

Demand Forecast
Restock Recommendation
Frequently Bought Together
Peak Sales Period

## PERFORMANCE

Use Django ORM efficiently.

Possible tools:

aggregate()
annotate()
Count()
Sum()
Avg()
Q()
F()
TruncDate()
TruncWeek()
TruncMonth()
select_related()
prefetch_related()

Avoid:

- N+1 queries
- one query per KPI card if unnecessary
- one query per chart point
- loading entire order tables into Python just to calculate aggregates

Use database aggregation when appropriate.

## CODEX WORKFLOW RULES

Whenever I ask you to modify CampusHub:

1. Read AGENTS.md if it exists.
2. Read CAMPUSHUB_CONTEXT.md if it exists.
3. Inspect relevant files first.
4. Trace frontend → API → model/database when needed.
5. Do not guess.
6. Reuse existing code.
7. Make the smallest safe change.
8. Preserve current business logic unless the task explicitly changes it.
9. Do not introduce duplicate endpoints/pages unnecessarily.
10. Do not overengineer.
11. Do not replace real data with mocks.
12. Do not redesign unrelated screens.

## CODE CHANGE REPORTING FORMAT

EVERY time you make or propose code changes, tell me:

Folder:
<folder>

File:
<file>

Line:
<approximate line>

Nearby anchor:
<class/function/template/component>

Then explain:

- What changed
- Why it changed
- Expected behavior
- How to test it
- Any limitation/assumption

Line numbers may move, so ALWAYS include a nearby code anchor.

## WHEN YOU NEED CLARIFICATION

Do not immediately ask me for information that can be discovered from the repository.

First inspect:

- models
- serializers
- views
- services
- URLs
- templates
- JavaScript
- CSS
- Flutter screens/controllers
- migrations
- tests

Ask me only when:

- a business policy is genuinely ambiguous
- multiple conflicting implementations exist
- institutional policy is not present in the repository
- an irreversible/destructive action requires confirmation

## FINAL INSTRUCTION

Before working on any task, briefly identify:

- which module is affected
- which files are likely involved
- which backend models/services need verification
- which existing business rules must remain intact

Then inspect the code.

Do not start by replacing entire files unless necessary.

Prefer precise, minimal, maintainable changes.

CampusHub business rules and actual repository implementation take priority over generic assumptions.
